1  Introduction

     1.1    Program Introduction

        The South Azadegan Oil field has been discovered by Well AZN-001, drilled in 1999.
       This well drilled to a total depth of 4,643m evaluating all the possible hydrocarbon bearing of
       the Cretaceous sequences. So far up to, 39 wells have been drilled in the South Azadegan Oil
        Field.
         Carbonate reservoir of Sarvak formation of the Middle Cretaceous age, was penetrated
      and evaluated in all the initial 6 exploration wells (AZN-001, AZN-002, AZN-003, AZN-
       004, AZN-005 and AZN-006) and has been broadly developed in Azadegan Oil Field.
      Moreover hydrocarbon potentials of the clastic oil bearing zones of Kazhdumi and Gadvan
      Formations were also evaluated in the early exploration phase of the field. Clastic oil bearing
      zones of Gadvan formation has only been developed in the crestal area and southern parts of
       the Azadegan Oil Filed.

     1.2    South Azadegan Structure

       As the Previous exploration/delineation drilling in Azadegan oil field has proved presence
       of  a  very  prolific  hydrocarbon  bearing  sedimentary column,  particularly  within  the
       Cretaceous sequences. The Fahliyan Formation was divided into 6 zones, named as Fah-0,
       Fah-1, Fah-2, Fah-3, Fah-4 and Fah-5, of which Fah-1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 bear light oil. The
       average thickness of the Fahliyan, based on well data, is 299m. The oil bearing zones of
       Fahliyan only developed in south part of the field around Well AZNS-004 and AZNS-006. 4
        wells, AZNS-004, 006, 010 and 011, penetrated Fahliyan oil bearing zones.

                              Figure 1: Azadegan Oil Field

         Pad-56 has been nominated as a  location  in which AZNS-022 (Sarvak  horizontal
       producer) well has been drilled, from this pad. F3 (Fahliyan vertical well) and S388 (Sarvak
       horizontal well), shall be drilled in this pad. This pad is located on middle of Azadegan oil
        field in Block II Error! Reference source not found. shows position of the Pad-56. The
       resent scope of work for Pad-56 location is to drill a vertical well (F3) in Fahliyan reservoir
      and subsequently to be completed as a Fahliyan carbonate reservoir oil producer.

   6 Key Reservoir Description

      Fahliyan Formation
    The Fahliyan Formation in south Azadegan Oil Field is generally composed of a sequence of
limestones,  off-white  to  cream  to  light  brown  wackestone-packstone,  occasionally  detrital,
fossiliferous and in places with intergranular and micro-vuggy. The Upper parts of the section, with
over 105 meters in thickness, as has been designated as Upper Fahliyan as has low reservoir quality.
    The Lower Fahliyan lower section is composed of clean, light cream to light brown limestone
with interbeds of dolomitic limestones. Towards the base the lithology changes into more argillaceous
limestone.

   9 Rig Move

             Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:
          o     Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.
          o     Safe standby areas for rig in case move have to be suspended.
          o     Loss of trucks in case of repair.
          o     Moving at night.
             Hold pre-move  safety  meeting.  Ensure  everyone  understands  individual
              Responsibilities.
            Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.
            Commence rig move to AZNS 045, F 03, PAD No. 56
              Position rig for optimal operations
             Rig up on cellar
              Finish rig up and commence operational procedure for AZNS 045
              Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.
              Test all manifold and surface safety valves
          o   RKB – GLE and Elevation
          o     Spud date and time.
            Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as
              possible.

   10    Pre Spud

1)  Certificate
              All rig equipment are on location, properly rigged up and function-tested.
      Check availability and content of hoisting equipment inspection & maintenance records
       according to API.
2) Safety Device
              Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report;
         test derrick man escape device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on
       daily drilling report; test rig intercom system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-
     Mud Logger communication link.
             Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g. fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations
         etc., is installed at the proper locations as per rig layout diagrams.
              Verify that substructure  is level and foundation adequate to support
        drilling unit during whole well period and that mast is centred above rotary table.
             Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig
        floor hoisting equipment are adequate for handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure
        that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have
      been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.
3) Rig Equipment
              Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed,
      and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools
        available for vertical section survey.
            Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been
        function-tested with charging pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate
       screen inventory for the shale shakers is available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure
       water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system are
      hooked up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section are on
        location, in sound condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and
        sufficient volume has been built for top section and for well-kill.
            Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts:
         liners, pistons, valves, seats  etc. Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with
       Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of expected standpipe pressure during
        drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available on location to
        adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off
       valves are set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge
        lines from the pop-off valves to the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-
       pressure hoses are properly secured with safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure
       surface line of circulating system.

4) Wellhead & BOP
             Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m
      above cellar bottom to drain cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface
        casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists
      between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.
            Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and
       ready to be run, float shoe should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on
       the pipe-rack prior to spud in the well.
             Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested
       properly.
5) Others
              Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig
      consumption available on site.
            Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is
       ok.
             Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location.
       Discuss procedures involved throughout 26″ hole section  drilling, reporting system and
         critical safety operation measures.

       NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed  it in rigs. The checklist will be

          performed based on the aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following

           matters should be checked:

            Check & observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in

                         four stages; rig levelling, derrick verticality, derrick alignment with rotary

                            table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3

                            joints of 9 1/2” DC and check the verticality of the derrick.

             Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as :

                      Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers,

                 mud guns & mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.

             All mud tanks, mud transfer lines & connection should be checked by

                              filling them with water & cross wellhead circulation.

            Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing

                       equipment. The calibration should be done under supervision of well site

                            drilling supervisor before starting the operation.

     1 STRUCTURAL GEOLOGY:

      The Azadegan oil field is located at 80km west of Ahwaz and the Azadegan Field is
 an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belongs to Arabian Trend which  is
 different from “Zagros Trend”. There is no outcrop for Azadegan structure and  it was
 detected by 3D seismic interpretation.

     2 LITHO-STRATIGRAPHY DESCRIPTION:

      The  stratigraphy sequence of  the  field includes Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari,
 Pabdeh,  Gurpi,  Ilam,  Laffan,  Sarvak,  Kazhdumi,  Dariyan,  Gadvan  and  Fahliyan
 Formations, from top to bottom. This sequence in well no. AZN-10, the deepest up to now
 drilled well of the field, continues with Garau, Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and
 Neyriz formations.
   Fars Group
    Fars group originally divided into two formations, Aghajari and Gachsaran formations.
   Aghajari Formation
     Depth: Surface to 1105m     Drilled Thickness: 1105m   Age: MIOCENE-PLIOCENE
 Lithology: This Formation begins with light cream, light gray, yellow, reddish brown,
 quartz, subrounded, good  sorted  Sandstone.  This  formation  is  mostly  consisting  of
 red marl interbedded with sandstone and red claystone layers. The Red claystone increase
 toward the base of the formation. The Sandstones are variably consolidated and generally
 light brown, light gray in colour, fine to medium grain size, generally fine grained, rarely
 coarse grained, generally  well  sorted  and  dominated by  sub-angular to  sub-rounded
 quartz  grains,  loose, friable to moderately hard, slightly calcareous. The Marls are red
 brown to brown and pale red brown, generally sticky and slightly silty, soft to firm and
 locally very low calcareous grading to claystone. The Red claystones are brown, moderate
 to dark brown, generally soft, sticky and amorphous, locally some calcareous grading to
 marl, generally non silty and occasionally mottled. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was
 observed, together with more marine fauna.
   Gachsaran Formation
     Depth: 1105-1454m      Drilled Thickness: 349m       Age: Middle Miocene - Early
 Miocene Lithology:  This Formation  begins with  white,  glassy,  soft, medium,  hard
 Anhydrite and  off white, light gray to gray, soft to medium hard, Dolomitic Limestone.
 Comprises of anhydrite interbedded with gray marl and red marl, salt beds  of  variable
 thickness and some thin limestone beds. This formation in type section not divided to other
 member. The Anhydrite is white off white, milky, soft, partly crystalline to crystalline,
 medium hard to hard. The Gray marl is gray to light gray, soft. The Red marl is red brown

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 24

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

 to brown, soft, sticky. The Limestone is gray to light gray, buff to buff gray, hard, slightly
 dolomitic.

  Asmari Formation
       Depth: 1454-1754m       Drilled Thickness: 300m         Age: Oligocene - Miocene
   Lithology: This Formation begins with cream, light gray, soft to medium hard, poor
    Fossil Dolomitic Limestone. Consisting of white, off white, light gray to gray, soft to
   medium hard,   I/II,I,   II,  Dolomitic  Limestone  particularly  in  upper  part.  The
   Sandstone increase toward the base with interbedded gray marl, dolomite and claystone
   of the beds formation. The Sandstone is gray, light to gray, medium to coarse with
    partly pyritic, medium sorted. The Gray marl is gray, light gray, soft. The Dolomite is
    light cream to cream, porous. The Claystone is red light brown, soft, sticky.

  Pabdeh Formation
        Depth: 1754-2184m      Thickness: 430m          Age: Paleocene-Eocene
   The Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field.

   Lithology:  This  formation  starts  with Marl,  gradually  replaces by  Argillaceous
   Limestone. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown Argillaceous Limestone and
   Marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is
   composed  of dark-brown,  slightly Argillaceous Limestone. Pelagic  fauna, mostly
   Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to
    late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene deposits exist in the Azadegan area.

  Gurpi
  Formation
   This  is formation consists of three main
    units:
   A) Upper Gurpi Member
   B) Tarbour Tongue
   C) Lower Gurpi Member

  Upper Gurpi Member
     Depth: 2184-2325m       Drilled Thickness: 141m      Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian

   Lithology: This Member begins with light gray, gray, off white, soft to medium, sticky
   Argillaceous limestone. The Upper Gurpi Member is mostly consisting of gray buff to
   gray with of  trace  partly buff  gray, dark  gray, medium hard  to hard  argillaceous,
   high argillaceous graded to marl Argillaceous limestone interbedded with anhydrite marl
  and chalky limestone beds. The Anhydritic marl is light gray to gray, soft, sticky, graded

   to high argillaceous calcareous. The Chalky Limestone is light gray to buff gray, medium
   hard to soft,chalky II,A slightly argillaceous.
   Tarbour Tongue
     Depth: 2325-2403m      Drilled Thickness: 78m     Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian
 Lithology: This Member begins with cream, light brown, porosity, oil stained Limestone.
 Tarbour Tongue consisting of cream to light gray, light brown, buff, medium hard, II,A
 Limestone interbedded with chalky limestone and marl beds. The Chalky limestone is
 light cream, soft. The Dolomite medium bedded dark gray. The Marl is light gray to gray,
 soft, sticky.

   Lower Gurpi Member
     Depth: 2403-2528 m      Drilled Thickness: 125m   Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian
 Lithology: This Member begins with off white, cream, soft to medium hard Argillaceous
 limestone and light gray to gray, soft, sticky Marl. The Member consisting of dark gray
 to gray, light gray, soft to medium hard Argillaceous limestone and light cream and soft.
 II, A. Chalky Limestone beds.

   Bangestan Group
    Bangestan group is divided into four formations: Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak and Kazhdumi.

   Ilam Formation
     Depth: 2528-2622        Drilled Thickness: 94m       Age: Coniacian -Santonian
 Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of limestone interbedded with shale and
 clay stone beds. The Limestone is cream, buff to buff gray, light brown, soft to medium
 hard, chalky, II, A, Oil stained Partly slightly argillaceous. The Shale is green gray to
 light green gray, mostly brown gray, medium hard, pyritic, slightly calcareous. The Clay
 stone is green gray to light green gray and soft, slightly calcareous.

   Laffan Formation
    Depth: 2622-2628 m         Drilled Thickness: 6m           Age: Coniacian

 Lithology: This Formation begins with greenish gray, green, Soft, sticky, pyrite, slightly
 and calcareous Claystone and green, light greenish gray, light gray, medium to hard, fissile
 Shale. This formation is mostly consisting of shale interbedded with clay stone beds. The
 Shale green gray to light green gray, mostly brown gray medium hard, pyritic, medium
 hard, slightly calcareous. The Clay stone green gray to light green gray, soft and slightly
 calcareous.

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

   Sarvak Formation
    Depth: 2628-3277m           Drilled Thickness: 649m        Age: Cenomanian

 Lithology: This Formation begins with light brown, cream, light cream, I/II, I and Oil
 stained Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of off white, cream, buff to cream
 and white to cream,  soft,  soft to medium hard, chalky Limestone interbedded with
 claystone, argillaceous limestone, shale, argillaceous dolomite and dolomite limestone
 beds. The Claystone is gray, soft, sticky, high calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is
 white gray to light gray, light buff, medium hard and slightly bituminous. The Shale is dark
 brown, dark gray, black, medium hard and calcareous. The Argillaceous Dolomite is buff
 gray, hard. The Dolomitic Limestone is buff gray to light brown gray, hard, densely. The
 Dolomitic Limestone is gray, partly light gray, medium hard.

   Kazhdumi Formation
Depth: 3277 - 3484m            Drilled Thickness: 207m                Age: Albian
Lithology: This Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and
calcareous Shale. This formation is start with dark brown to dark green gray, medium hard to
hard, soft to medium, high bituminous, calcareous Shale interbedded with argillaceous
limestone,  sandy  claystone,  claystone,  marl,  sandy  limestone,  limestone  beds.  The
Argillaceous Limestone  is dark brown  to brown  gray, medium hard  to  hard, micro
bituminous. The Sandy claystone is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is
gray to light gray, soft. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Limestone is dark
green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to green gray, medium hard to soft,
partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadeghan Member.

Khami Group
 Khami group is divided into three formations: Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan.

Dariyan Formation
Depth: 3484 - 3645m                 Drilled Thickness: 161m              Age: Aptian
Lithology: This Formation begins with off white, light cream, I/II, mottled Limestone. This
formation is mostly consisting of white to light cream, off white, white to light gray, medium
hard Limestone interbedded with light gray to gray, light gray, green gray medium hard to
hard, partly crystalline carbonaceous, pyritic, partly sandy Argillaceous Limestone beds.
Gadvan Formation
Depth: 3645 - 3716m             Drilled Thickness: 71m      Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN
Lithology: This formation is mostly consisting dark green gray to green gray, soft, sticky,
partly calcareous Shale and light gray, green gray, soft, sticky Claystone interbedded with
argillaceous limestone, marl, sandstone, sandy claystone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone
is green gray to light gray to white, pyritic hard. The Marl is green gray, soft and sticky. The

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 27

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

Sandstone  is brown, gray to brown, sorted, rounded, hard, calcareous, oil stained. The
Sandyclaystone is light gray, soft and with quartz grains.
Khalij Member
Depth: 3716 - 3727m           Drilled Thickness: 11m       Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN
Lithology: The member comprises of light gray to gray, medium hard, partly pyritic
Argillaceous Limestone and white, white to light gray, hard Limestone beds.
lower Gadvan Formation
Depth: 3727- 3933m       Drilled Thickness: 206m      Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN
Lithology: This formation  is  consisting of  alternating of  marl,  argillaceous  limestone,
limestone, claystone, sandy claystone and sandstone beds. The Marl is green gray, soft,
sticky, slightly calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is gray white gray medium hard to
soft, partly pyritic. The Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is light
gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky, fine sand grained. The
Sandstone is brown to gray, partly white buff, gray, friable, sorted, subrounded,medium
quartz grained, partly calcareous.
Fahliyan Formation
Depth: 3933 - 4125m          Drilled Thickness: 192m             Age: NEOCOMIAN
Lithology: This formation  is  start with  light  gray,  light green  gray,  soft Limestone
interbedded with green gray, soft, sticky Marl, green gray, light gray, soft, Argillaceous
Limestone and dark green gray, soft, sticky Claystone beds. This formation at continue is
consisting of buff white light gray, light brown, brown, medium hard, hard, densely, slightly
argillaceous Limestone and off white, white gray, medium hard Chalky Limestone beds.

Note:

  Full Suite Logs include GR, Sonic/DSI, Spectral GR, Resistivity, Neutron, Density, and PEF
     logs.

 The CBL/VDL log will be performed after running and cementing, 9-5/8” casing from casing
    shoe to surface, and after cementing 7” liner, the CBL/VDL logging will be run from the shoe to
     liner hanger.

  In 12-1/4” hole size, GR/DLL/MSFL /Sonic will be run from TD to 13-3/8” casing shoe.

 MDT points maybe changed after petrophysical logs review.

 DSI log will be run in only reservoir zones.

  Deviation tool could be run in all hole sizes based on Well-Site Geologist or Company man
     request.

17 ½ " HOLE SECTION (119M- 1538M) SUMMERY:

    Drilled by 17 ½” bit and 65-80 pcf mud weight in Gachsaran formation to the depth of 1226 m with

problem at 1300 m and passed by W/R and pumped 100 bbl Hi.Vis Pill with 6 bph lost. Then, set the 13

3/8” casing at 1538 m and cemented with (98 bbl, 115 pcf) "A" class.

12 ¼ ″ HOLE SECTION (1538M-1930M) SUMMERY:

    Drilled by 12 ¼” bit and 80-112 pcf mud weight in Gachsaran formation to 1602 with NL/NG.

Continued drilling to the depth of 1570 m and suddenly bit took weigh so increase mud weight from 80 to

88 pcf. Increased MW to 95 pcf at 1610 m due to oil contaminated mud and salt water on shaker and
increase Ca++ and PH in return mud. Because of tight hole problem in 1634, 1690 and 1750 m increased

mud weight finally to 110 pcf. Then, took LCS/GR/BGN/CDR logs from 1928 to 1500. Ran 9 5/8” casing

at the depth of 1930 and cemented by ‘D’ class (550 bbl, 120 pcf) cement.

8 ⅜" HOLE SECTION (1930M- 3916M) SUMMERY:

    Drilled by 8 3/8” bit and 74 pcf mud weight in Asemari formation. Continue drilling to 2250 m in

Pabdeh, Gurpi, and Ilam and Laffan formations with NL/NG. Took 3.5 m core from Sarvak formation at

2826 m. then, displaced water base mud with 76 pcf oil base mud and continued drilling to 2854 m which

drill string was stuck and got free by 50 klb over pull. Increased MW to 80 pcf and took 17 and 25 m core

from 2854-2871 and 2890-2917.5 m intervals. Continued drilling to 3075 with 2-10 bph lost so pumped

Hi.Vis Pill periodically. Took DLL/MSFL/GR/LDT/CNL/NGS/BHC from 3087 to 1900 m. continued

drilling Kazhdumi and Darian formations to 3618 m and observed oil contaminated and gas saturated in

return mud so, circulated to balance Mw to 76 pcf. Continued drilling to in Gadvan formation and

observed Gas cut mud in 3980m and 4107 m so increased MW to 84 and 86 pcf. Drilled to 4051m and

due to gas cut mud and decreased MW had to increase and circulated mud with weight 110 pcf but Not

Succeed    so,    set   bridge   plug   and   squeezed   32   bbl   ‘D’   cement.   Then   took

DLL/MSFL/BHC/GR/LDL/CNL/NGT Logs from 3916m to 2965 m and RIH RFT tools and recorded 72

points pressure. Finally ran 7” Liner and hanged from 1835.5 m to 3916 m and cemented by 977 sks ‘D’

cement.

   2. Lesson learned

   Most of problems in south Azadegan field occurred in 3th and 4th interlayer of Gachsaran Group due

to presence of sticky Red and Brown Marl. Also, reorganization of first interlayer of Gachsaran which

into account cap rock of Asmari reservoir is so important to prevent lost and stuck problem through low

pressure pore formation pressure.

    Further shale and clay swelling in Bangestan Group is customary.

    Understanding and anticipating drilling problems, understanding their causes, and planning solutions

are necessary for overall-well-cost control and for successfully reaching the target zone. According to

report of nearby wells, the following items has been considered to drilling the new well:

    Bit balling is common problem. A rough bit surface will increase the surface area and increase the

adhesive forces. When the mud flow stops, or if a bit with poor hydraulic design is used, an electrostatic

force may cause clays to stick in the bit surface. With several work such as increase flow rate, Pump a

caustic pill, Spin the bit as fast as possible can be prevented this problem.

    Stuck  is another problem in the previous wells. Stuck can be classified under two categories:

differential and mechanical. In the nearby wells, it can be said that Mechanical type wasn’t happened

except stuck 13 3/8” casing in AZN 047 at 765m (Aghajari formation and got free with 250 Klbs

overpull). More stuck in the mentioned wells often is associated with complete loss, so differential type

may be happened. In AZN 047, string got stuck with 71 PCF mud from 1928m to 1945m (Pabdeh

formation) and got free with 40 Klbs Over pull. Another stuck in this well was happened with 76 PCF

mud at 2675m to 2714m (Sarvak Formation) and got free with 60-100 Klbs over pull.

    Tight hole is a common problem in the mentioned wells and usally for curing this problem, mud

weight is increased. The serius tight hole was reported in Jahrum/Pabdeh and Gurpi. It should be noted

that when tight hole is cured, mud weight don’t excess from critical value.

   The costly problem that may be happened in future wells is twist off string. Twisting off is ocurred

when the drill string parts by failing catastrophically under the torsional stress. This may happen if the

drill string has been encountered either fatigue or excessive torque. This problem has been occurred four

times in AZN 025(2660m, 3445m, 3472m and 3542m) and fish was recovered. Because of string got

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 44

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

stuck, twist off bit sub was happened in Jahrum/Pabdeh formation. Due to fish hasn’t been recovered; side

tracking was performing from 1533m.

17 ½” Problems and Solutions:

    In 17 ½″ hole section that had been drilled Aghajari-Gachsaran formation, tight hole was encounter

when during the condition trip, wash and ream several times this interval. Totally operation is smoothly, a

proper mud weight in this section is important for the well controlling operations. Also Hi.Vis pill

pumping make the hoe condition better. This problem had been occurred in two situations:

            Bite toot wearing: if this problem depended on bit wearing should redisigned the bit

              selection proucedure and review the bit performance history in this interval.adidithnally

               in rather ran short drilling test and calculated Af for next bit selection

           Shale and saltted formation: If tight hole occurred under shale and clay sweilling or

               saltted formation influence, should increase the mud wight to prevent pipe stuck.

           Thickness of mud cake: sometimes uncontrled grownig the mud cack is a factor cuase

               to tight hole problem. The signe of this condithion is decreasing the sample on mud

              returned. To solve this problem must contole water loos.

    In other hand, increasing the Ca++ near the 1100m in Gachsaran formation occurred that solved in

drilling opration by increasing mud weight. Since as important factor which influence on mud reology and

espically on plastic vicosity is hole cleaning from drilling cutting. If cutting removing don’t as suffienet,

cuttings remained in bore hole and redrilled by bit and be smaller. Smaller cutting cuase to increas the

active surfaces and tottaly increas friction force between side surface and increasing plastic viscosity. The

treat of  this problem seprat into two way; solid control system included cenrefigue and chimacal

treatment. In chemical treatment for removing Ca++ and Mg++ should be use of Posphat Complex and

Ligno Sulfanite as Emulusifiers or Ditution the mud.

12 ¼” Problems and Solutions:

    In 12 ¼ ″ hole section that had been drilled Gachsaran formation, lost circulation in 2800m-2900m

interval happened and solved by LCM squeezing and Hi.Vis pumping. About lost circulation the main

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 45

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

mud with water and addetives in first step should recognize which kind of losting categories happent by

mud levling from pumped and retuned mud tanks. Referr to this table:

                 To 25 bph      Speeage   Pourose and permeable media like sandstones
                                                formation

                   25-100 bph         partial            Coars, sands and Gravel formation

              Up to 100 bph      Sever             Fractured and faulted formation

               If cuold not perevent the lost by LCM can use of magne-set program.

              For sever  lost  circulatio should use cementing but not in reservoir becuease

                 influenced on important factor of of reservoir specification which is permeability.

               It is recommended that the pump rate is no more than 500gpm in formations which

                  are vulnerable to lost circulation such as Asmari and Gurpi. Meanwhile, mud

                weight shall not be changed sharply.

   One of the dangerous problems that occurred during drilling this section is observing cut mud weight

in 2200m to 2400 m interval. This problem has been solved by increasing the mud weight and circulated

to balance it.

    Bit balling is another problem that happened during drilling 17 ½″ and 12 ¼″ hole section in Aghajari

and Gachsaran formation. The best approach is to use good fundamentals: an inhibitive mud, optimal

hydraulics and a quality ROP enhancer. Cure bit balling by following items:

       Work the bit by rotating fast off bottom and reciprocating the string to shake off the

              clay coating.

       Reduce ROP for a short time.

       Spot a walnut pill.

       Circulation.

       Fresh water.

       If bit balling is observed while drilling, a detergent is usually added to disperse the

               balled clays.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 46

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

       The use of a small percentage of glycol also helps to reduce bit balling.

       Consider fresh water pill.

8 ½" Problems and Solutions:

    Kick problem occurs in Gadvan formation near the 4000m to 4640m and solved by increasing the

mud weight. For solving this problem, Recalculation mud weight for each problematic interval based on

fracture gradient, pore pressure and compressive strength of those interval rocks should be performed to

prepare suitable mud and overbalanced with minimum 200 psi more than pore pressure.

1  26” Hole Section / 20” Surface Casing

      1.1 General

       Casing Point            20” Casing @ 150m (20”, 94#, K-55, BTC)
       Formation                Aghajari
        Risks & Hole problems   No Significant Hole Problem
        Deviation control        Totco
       Logging                       ---
                                  Bentonite Mud
                MW    65-68 pcf
        Drilling Fluid
                                     Vis.    70-80 sec
                        PH    ≥ 10.5
                   WOB  Maximum Available
        Drilling Parameters    GPM   600-1100
                     RPM   90-130

      1.2 Formation Sampling
        On each 5 meters one sample would be taken. In addition to this general rule, geologist
        may decide to take more samples.

      1.3 Sequence of Operation

             Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design, RIH.
             Tag bottom of cellar, start controlled drilling. Drill the first and second stands at
              60-70 RPM with low flow rate (300 GPM) to avoid washing out the conductor
                pipe.
             Pump Hi-Vis pill to sweep hole when every stand drilled down. Continue drilling to
             ±150 m (20”casing point in Aghajari formation). Pay enough attention to  drill
                  straight hole. Use LCM pill if encountered heavy losses. If loss couldn’t be cured by
         LCM pill next program will be issue later.

           Note:
                - TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole under casing shoe.
                - The exact TD will be determined based on 20”casing tally.
                - Do not forget to strap pipe while POOH.
                - Run Totco each 50 m. to evaluate the verticality of hole.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 49

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

                - Use following parameters to drill this hole section:
            -WOB = Maximum available {Pay enough attention to drill straight hole}
             -RPM = 90-130
             -GPM = 600-1100

             At TD, Circulate hole clean. POOH to surface. Check  bit  if balling. Perform
                condition trip. Spot Hi-Vis pills after circulate hole clean. Drop Totco, POOH and
                  retrieve survey tool.
             Hold pre-job safety meeting with involved personnel for running 20″ casing.

             R/U 20” casing running gear & function test 20″ casing running equipment. Perform
                Safety meeting.

             Match the stab-in stinger with float shoe on surface.

             P/U and M/U shoe joint.

             Thread-lock shoe connection. Function test float shoe, Fill up casing with mud every
                  joint to keep weight balance between inside casing and annular. Monitor WOH
                during running casing to detect excessive drag.

             Run ±150m (Approx. 15 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in type float
               shoe to bottom as per casing program (install centralizers as per casing tally). Casing
               shoe should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough stick
              up (1.5 m) on rig floor. Fill casing by non LCM fresh mud joint by joint.

             Rig up C-plate. Run stab-in stinger (with new O-ring seal), 5″ pup joint (with 5″ x
              20″ bow spring centralizer) and 5″ HWDP as stinger string in 20″ casing
             Slowly lower stinger string and make sure stab into float shoe ok.
             Make up circulating head. Fill up annulus (5″ x 20″) with mud and check for sealing.
             Secure the inner string to the rig substructure with slings.
             Break circulation slowly. And circulate 200% of annular volume with mud at 800-
              1000 GPM.

           Note:
                - Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges
               are encountered.

             Hook up cement line and pressure test same (Test Pressure: 3000 psi).
             Hold pre job safety meeting with related people.
             Cement 20″ casing as per cement program.
             P/U stinger, circulate to flush string inside. POOH.
             WOC.

────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

           Note:
                - Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top-up
              jobs.

              If there is no cement return to surface, a top-up job should be performed to fill the
                annulus, stage by stage until cement reaches the surface. Use the same weight as the
               primary cement slurry. Ensure adequate X-Over for connecting line pipe to cement
                  line is available. Wait for the top job cement to get hard.

             Correctly determine elevation from top flange of CHH to RKB and then Rough cut
              20” landing joint and L/D same. L/D and remove 30” conductor pipe.

             Dress casing neck suitable to install and to weld casing head spool. (If C.H.H is slip
                type at bottom, install it as per Wellhead Engineer Installation).

              Install and weld casing head housing 20 3/4” – 3000 psi on casing stub and test the
                 sealing of welded casing (limit pressure to a maximum of 80% of the collapse
                 strength of the casing or maximum 400 psi). Outlets to align with cellar stairs.

             N/U spacer, kill/choke manifold and 20-3/4″ 3K Hydrill.

             P/T wellhead & BOP system as per NIOC Policy.

              Install wear bushing.

           Note:
                  - Do not forget to check the wear bushing with CHH and tie down flange before N/U
            BOP’s.
             Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:
                         20” Casing Tally
                         20” Casing Report
                         20” Cement Report
                         26” Hole Section Summary
                         Survey Report
               WHD Test report &  it’s diagram including dimensions and  serial
                          numbers.

2  17-1/2” Hole Section / 13-3/8” Intermediate Casing

      2.1 General

       Casing Point             13-3/8” Casing @ 1449m (68#, N-80, BTC)
       Formation                 Aghajari, Gachsaran, 5 m above top of Asmari
                                   Loss, Salt water influx, Calcium contamination & tight
        Risks & Hole problems
                                  hole
                              GR/CALI/Deviation Tool (Single shot every 100m and
       Logging                                   multi shot every 300m)

                       PH     ≥ 10.5

                       PH      9.5 - 10
                   WOB   10 - 50 klb
        Drilling Parameters     RPM   90-150
                     GPM   900-1100

      2.2 Formation Sampling
           From 150m to section TD: On each 2-5 meters one sample would be taken.
             Note: As per well site geologist request, the sample program may be changed.

      2.3 Sequence of Operations

             M/U and RIH with 17-1/2″ drilling BHA.
             Tag TOC. Circulate. Drill out cement and shoe track with reduced parameters.
              Drill with reduced parameters till stabilizer has passed through casing shoe.
             Continue drill 3-5m new formation.
              Circulate hole clean It. POOH to casing shoe.
             Perform FIT as per procedure (EMW= Max hole MW+2 PCF).
             Continue drilling to 300 m, Displace new mud into hole.
              Drill ahead with optimized parameters.
             Short trip every 300m or depending on hole condition.
              Drill hole to section TD, perform wiper trip, circulate, spot Hi-Vis pill if necessary,
                take survey and POOH.

                - Casing shoe should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough
              stick up (1.5 m) on rig floor.

      3.3 Sequence of Operations

             M/U & RIH 12-1/4″ BHA to tag TOC.
              Circulate. Drill plugs, float collar and cement to 7m above float shoe with reduced
                parameters.
             Displace new mud and circulate to condition same.
              If observed NO bumping while 13 3/8” cement job, Circulate and uniform mud then
               performed P/T 13 3/8” casing integrity test to 1500 PSI.

       4.3 Sequence of Operations

               Drill out cement to 7m above float shoe with reduced parameters.
              Displace new mud and circulate to condition same.
               If observed NO bumping while 9 5/8” cement job, Circulate and uniform mud then
               performed P/T 9 5/8” casing integrity test to 3000 PSI.
              Continue drill out cement and shoe.
              Commence drilling formation with reduced parameters.
              Continue drill 3-5 m of formation.
               Circulate clean the hole. POOH to casing shoe.

2  26" Hole Section

 2.1 Section Discussion

   The 26” hole is planned to drill with Bentonite Hi Vis. Mud to Section TD at 150 m to run 20”

    surface casing.

    After drilling of each stand, 40 bbl. of High viscous pill should be pumped to clean hole or more

      if the hole condition dictates. Once TD is reached, pump 80 bbl. of high viscous pill, circulate it

    out and prior to pull out the BHA for running the 20" casing,

    General practices to help avoiding hole to pack off during connection:
             Circulate out the whole with 100 bbl. Hi-Vis pill prior to any Totco run in hole
             Prior to POOH for BHA change, spot sufficient amount of Hi-Vis pill at bottom

3  17 1/2" Hole Section

 3.1 Section Discussion

   The 17 1/2” hole section will be drilled from 20" casing shoe at 150 ± m to an estimated depth of

   1449± m. The  interval  consists of two formations, Aghajari, Gachsaran formations. The

    formations are predominately made up of Sandstone, Clay stone, Marl, and anhydrate.

    This section will be drilled down using bentonite mud to 300 m from previous section. After

     drilling 300 m interval, mud should be dumped and the hole will be displaced with KCl  /

    Polymer system and drilling continues to the depth of 1449m (MD).

    Before entering Gachsaran formation density should reach to 75 PCF and fluid loss control is the

     critical issue while drilling this section and should be maintained as program.

3.2  Drilling Hazards
    Wellbore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

        * Swelling clays

          *Partial losses

 3.3 Hole instability

    In addition to KCl mud and PHPA can be added to provide extra inhibition in clay section.

   PHPA acts as encapsulating polymer, minimizing the hydration dispersion of cutting. Pump low

    Vis and hi-vis. pill periodic

 3.4 Lost circulation

   The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. In the event of seepage losses, treat

    system with a fine to medium grade of LCM at 10 lb. /bbl. to 20 lb. /bbl. In the event of moderate

     to high rate of wellbore loss, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot over thief

    zone. Concentration of LCM may need to recalculate.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                               Page 70

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

 3.6 Mixing procedures

   3.6.1   Interval of 150-300 m

        1. Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

        2. Treat out any hardness greater than 600 ppm with soda ash (1-3b/bbl).

        3. Add 25-30lb/bbl. of bentonite and allow hydrating for as long as possible

        4. Raise PH to 10.5- 11.5 with additional of caustic soda (0.5-2 lb. /bbl.)

   3.6.2   Interval of 300-1449 m

    After drilling up to 300 m, displace the previous mud with the new mud:

  1.  Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water

  2.  Add 0.3%-0.5% anti-bacterial

  3.  Treat out any hardness greater than 800 ppm with soda ash 1-3 ppb

  4. Add KCl for shale inhibitor to reach %4-%6 w/w about 14.4-21.6 ppb.

  5. Add NaCl to M.W 74 PCF.

  6. Add Green starch 9 -11 ppb then wait 10 to 15 minute on mixing

  7. Use 1-2 ppb PAC-L and wait 5 minute

  8. Use 0.5-1 ppb PAC-R and wait 5 minute

  9. Use Limestone to weight up above 74 PCF as needed

 10. Add about 0.5-2 ppb caustic soda to control pH and reduce hardness, PH value should not

     exceed more than 10.

Notices
  1. Make PHPA pill in reserve tank w/0.5 - 1 ppb solution and add to system while drilling

  2.  If use of potato starch cannot control F.L replace it with Modified or HT starch.

  3.  At 900 MT F.L should be reduce to 10 cc/30 min.

  4. Use solid control equipment while drilling (Especially the centrifuges).

  5. Add materials one by one as shown same as above.

  6. Pump Low Vis and Hi vis. pill as need.

4  12 1/4" Hole Section

 4.1 Section Discussion

   The 12 1/4" hole section will be drilled from 13 3/8" casing shoe at 1449± m to an estimated

    section TD of 3933± m (MD).

   The 9 5/8" casing shall cover Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Darian,

    and Gadvan formations. The fluid from previous section will be carried and utilization of a

    Glycol/ KCl / Polymer mud system (add glycol to active previous mud system) and should firmly

     inhibit shale and provides excellent properties drilling and inhibition properties.

4.2  Drilling Hazards
     Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

           * Hole instability

           * Salt water influx

   4.2.1   Hole Instability

      It is important to maintain mud weight as per pore pressure curve dictates in the drilling program.

    Ensure the concentrations are as per program and that these are determined and reported every

     tour. This should provide optimum hole instability and less hole problem.

   4.2.2  High formation loss

      If observe high amount of loss in formation, drilling should be performed in By-Pass mode.

    Additionally, LCMs are also added to the mud system. After finishing drilling of hi loss

    formation zone maybe pumping sodium silicate and cement slurry should be performed as per

     instruction.

4.3  Drilling mud

             Chemical                                       Function

               Water                              should have less than 800 ppm calcium

                    Salt                                       Weighting agent

             Soda ash                                     Hardness control

          Potassium chloride                                            Inhibitor

             Starch (Green)                                      Fluid loss control

              Limestone                                     Weighting agent

          PAC LV                                         Fluid loss control

        Xanthan Gum (xc pol.)                                Rheological control

              Caustic soda                            PH control

                Glycol                                                  Inhibitor

 4.4 Mixing procedure

    1.  Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water
    2.  Treat out any hardness greater than 700 ppm with soda ash
    3. Add KCl for shale inhibitor to reach %7 - 8% w/w (about 25.6 – 29 ppb)
    4. Add NaCl to weight up fluid to 74PCF
    5. Add 10 - 12 ppb starch HT and let to be mixed for 10 to 15 minute
    6.  use 1 - 2 ppb PAC-L and mix it for at least 5 minute
    7.  use Limestone to weight up above 74 PCF as needed
    8.  add 0.5 - 1 ppb Xc-polymer as needed
    9.  Shall use %3 - %5 v/v Glycol to active system. (Before using coordinate with office)
    10. Add about 0.3 - 1 ppb caustic soda to control pH and reduce hardness, PH value should not
       exceed more than 10.
   Notice:
    1. Use solid control equipment while drilling (Especially the centrifuges).
    2.  If need Add Bit Lubricant solution (%2 - %3 v/v), (Before use coordinate with office).

5  8 1/2" Hole Section

 5.1 Section Discussion

   The 8 1/2" hole section will be drilled from 9 5/8" casing shoe at 3933 ± m to an estimated

    section TD of 4125 ± m (MD). The 7" liner shall cover Fahliyan formation. The fluid from

    previous section will be carried and utilization of a KCl/Glycol/Polymer mud system and

    increase mud weight for 8 ½” hole and should firmly inhibit and provides excellent properties

     drilling and inhibition properties.

 5.2  Drilling Hazards

     Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

         * Possible losses

         * Present of H2S

   5.2.1   Lost circulation

   The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Partial to severe losses are expected in

    Sarvak formation and therefore sufficient stocks of acid soluble graded LCM, such as fine

   CaCO3 and oyster shell should be kept on board. In case of seepage losses, the system should be

     treated with medium grade of LCM. For moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different

    graded concentrations and spot across the thief zone.

   5.2.2   Presence of H2S

    Treat system with H2S scavenger, keep high alkalinity and use Garret gas train to determine

    concentration.

6  Solid control

1) Each kind of solid control equipment should be designed to process 100% to 150% of the
   maximum anticipated circulating volume of mud. Shale shaker, de-sander, de-silter and cleaner
    are required to run all the time during drilling and centrifuge is recommended to run at least 80%
    of drilling time.

2) The sand content and MBT should be kept as low as possible by solid removal and dilution. Batch
   wise dilution (replacement of 20-30% of the total drilling fluid volume in one circulation) is more
    effective than continuous dilution.

3) The detailed requirement of solid control equipment are as follows:

                            The capacity of Solids Control Equipment

7  Guidelines for Kill mud preparation
1) The kill fluid should be prepared prior to commence of each hole section, If well flow observed.

The density of kill fluid is determined usually heavier than the formation coefficient should be

recalculated this coefficient by new situation. Volume of kill fluid is following the regulation of

South Azadegan Oil Field:

For pore pressure coefficient of formation increase and pay zone without H2S, the volume of kill mud

will changed and need to recalculate.

If in case pore pressure coefficient of formation increase and pay zone has H2S, the volume of kill

mud will changed and need to recalculate.

2) The following emergency or contingency materials should be kept on the rig site at all times to

supplement the proposed mud system. Kick, lost circulation, stuck pipe scenarios, lubrication and

general purpose operations may require the following products to be on storage or standby.

                                        Kill Fluid and Chemical Storage
                       Section (inch)        26     17½    12 ¼"   8 ½"
             Max Pore P. coefficient    1.18     1.26     1.26      1. 6
              LCM (Tone)              -       15      15      10
                      Pipe Lax (DR)             -       10      10      10

8  Formation or Pore Pressure and Mud Weight
Primary Well Control relies on the hydrostatic pressure of drilling fluid to balance the formation pore

pressure. In order to counteract any minor pressure reductions on bottom due to swabbing effect when

tripping, it is customary to maintain a hydrostatic column with a pressure slightly exceeding the pore

pressure of the exposed formation. This overbalance, safety margin, or trip margin, Ptm, varies

depending on circumstances but it is generally of the order of 200 psi for conventional operations.

The required density Pf to control a formation at a depth, D, with a pressure, Po, is given by: Pf =

(Po+Ptm), or Df = (Po+Ptm)/D

To gas cut or contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.07-0.15) sg,

To oil and/or salt water contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.05-0.10) sg;

4  Casing running procedure

      4.1 26” Hole Section / 20” Surface Casing
String Design
 20” Casing Stab-in Float Shoe, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC.

 ±150m (Approx.13 Jts) 20” Casing, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of CSG=12m) to

   Surface.

 26” x 20” bow Centralisers on each 2 Joints.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the Stab-in Float Shoe for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment are available and in good operation condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted.

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of Operations
 Conduct safety meeting with all crews involved.

 Rig up casing handling and running equipment.

 P/U 20”, 94 ppf, K-55, BTC, float shoe, soft make up on first joint, P/U and make up to the optimum

   torque. Do not engage the tong close to bottom of shoe. Use thread lock compound for shoe.

 Run 20” casing & centralisers according to tally.

 Run casing at speed calculated by WSS. A rate of 5 Joints/hr. could be used as a guide.

 Work slightly through any tight spots if necessary. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Fill casing while running in each joint.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 103

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

      4.2 17-1/2" Hole Section / 13 3/8” Intermediate Casing
String Design
 13-3/8” Casing float Shoe, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 Two Joints (Approx. ±24m) of 13-3/8” Casing, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 13-3/8” Casing float Collar, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC.

 ±1425m (Approx. ±119Jts) of 13-3/8” Casing, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of CSG=12m).

 Bow Centralizer on the first 4 joints used every joint.

 Bow Centralizers 1 ea. every four joints.

 Rigid centralizer on third and fourth joints from top.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the float shoe and collar for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment are available and in good condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Check the plugs fins not to be squeezed or damaged.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted. Including 13 3/8”swedge (If required to wash casing down).

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure. Ensure that the air supply for cement is functioning properly.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of operations

 Rig up casing handling and running equipment.

 M/U 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC, float shoe. If it is not already made up on joint, soft make up on first

    joint, P/U and make up to the optimum make up torque based on triangle using casing tong. Do not engage

   the tong close to bottom of shoe. Repeat the same for collar.

 Use thread locking compound for shoe track plus two joints on top of float collar. Take special care on

   thread locking these joints, since the consequence of improper locking would be disastrous.

 Run two joints of 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, N-80, BTC casings.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 104

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

 Make up 13 3/8”, 68 ppf, BTC, float collar.

 Run Approx. ±1425m (±119 Jts.) of 13 3/8” Casing, 68ppf, N-80, BTC. (Est. length for each joint of

  CSG=12m).

Note: Circulate through casing after running of totally 5 Jts. for one Casing capacity.

 Note: Manage to have one meters of rat hole and 1.5 meters of casing stick up on rig floor.

 Pump one and half the casing volume @ 20” shoe and on TD.

 Run casing at speed calculated by Company Man. a rate of 10 Joints/hr. could be used as a guide.

 Using stabbing board & single joint elevator to run casing would be time saving.

 Work slightly through any tight spots. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Precautionary wash the last joint.

 When landed the casing, M/U cement head. Load the plugs accordingly. Check the flag to operate properly.

      4.3 12-1/4" Hole Section / 9 5/8” Production Casing
String Design

   9 5/8” Casing float Shoe, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

   Two Joints (Approx.±24m) of 9 5/8” Casing, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

   9 5/8” float Collar, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

   ±3909m (Approx. ±326 Joints) of 9 5/8” Casing, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ (Est. length for each joint of

     CSG=12m).

    Bow Centralizer on the first 4 joints.

    Bow Centralisers 1 ea every four joints.

     Rigid centralisers on third and fourth joints from top.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the shoe & collar for proper operation.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment is available and in good condition.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 105

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Check the plugs fins not to be squeezed or damaged.

 Ensure that all casing joints have been drifted, including 9 5/8”swedge (If required to wash casing down).

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure. Ensure that the air supply system for cement is functioning properly.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of Operations
 Rig up casing handling and running equipment, (Including jam system).

 P/U 9 5/8 float Shoe, 47 ppf,110S, TPCQ, and M/U to the optimum make up torque. Do not engage the

    tong close to bottom of shoe.

 Use thread locking compound for shoe track plus two joints on top of collar.

 Run two joints of 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ, casing.

 Make up 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ, float collar.

 Run Approx. ±3909m (±326 Joints) of 9 5/8”, 47 ppf, 110S, TPCQ casing.

   Note: Circulate through casing after running of totally 5 Jts for one Casing capacity.

 Manage to have 1m of rat hole and 1.5 meters of casing stick up on rig floor.

 Pump a complete casing volume at shoe and on the section TD.

 Run casing at speed calculated by Company Man. A rate of 10 Jts/hr. could be used as a guide.

 Work slightly through any tight spots if necessary. Circulate if deemed necessary.

 Precautionary wash the last joint.

 When landed the casing, M/U cement head. Load the plugs accordingly. Check the flag to operate properly.

      4.4 8 1/2" Hole Section / 7” Liner
String Design
 7” Float shoe, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

 Two Joints (Approx.±24m) of 7” Liner, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

 7” Float Collar, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

 One Joint (Approx.±12m) of 7” Liner, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 106

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

 7” LDC, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

 ±262m (Approx. ±22 Joints) of 7” Liner, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ. (Est. length for each joint of Liner=12m).

 7” x 9 5/8” Liner Hanger.

 Liner hanger setting tools.

 Approximately 3863m of 5” DP to surface.

 Rotating Centralizer on the first 4 joints used every joint.

 Rotating centralizer every 3 joints.

 7” Rotating centralizer on the third, fourth, fifth and sixth joint from the top.

Preparation
 Visually and mechanically check the shoe & collar for proper operation.

 Check that all consumable liner equipment, running tools, darts and plugs are on location.

 Ensure all connections are thoroughly cleaned using steam or a grease solvent.

 Ensure that all casing running equipment is available and in good condition.

 Ensure the correct casing drifts are supplied to the rig.

 Check the plugs fins not to be squeezed or damaged.

 Ensure all cement bulk lines have been checked for blockage with bulk tanks fluffed and pressurized to the

   required transfer pressure. Ensure that the air supply system for cement is functioning properly.

 Inspect and pressure test cement manifold.

Sequence of Operations
   1.  WSS is to supervise the loading of liner wiper plug in liner running tool, and loading of drill pipe

       wiper dart in cementing head.

   2.  The liner hanger engineer is to be on the rig floor at all times that the liner is being run in open hole.

   3.   Hold pre-job safety meeting. When running-in, apply thread lock to all connections of the shoe track.

   4.  Rig up liner running equipment. ,(Including jam system).Run 7” liner string considering the

       following arrangements:

   7”, 32ppf, 110S, TPCQ., float shoe.

   Two joints (Approx. 24m) of 7”, 32ppf, 110S, TPCQ..

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 107

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

   7”, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ., float collar.

   One joints (Approx. 12m) of 7”, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ.

   7”, 32 ppf, 110S, TPCQ. Latch down collar.

   Approx. ±262m (±22 Joints) 7”, 32ppf, 110S, TPCQ Liner.

          (Est. length for each joint of liner=12m).

   Hydraulic set liner hanger + liner top packer.

   Liner setting sleeve C/W liner setting tool connected

   5”, 19.5 lb/ft., drill pipe to surface.

Centralizers:
         - 1 rotating centralizers for the first 4 joints (1ea per joint).

          - 1 rotating centralizer per 3 joint for the rest of the open hole.

          - 1 rotating centralizer per 3 joint for the rest liner joints inside 9 5/8” casing.

          - Use rotating centralizer on the third and fifth and sixth joint from the top.

       Place each centralizer between 2 No. stop collars.

   5.   After making up the shoe track, lower through rotary table and check that the floats are holding. Fill up

       with mud and pick up to check float functionality. Circulate for 10 min.

   6.  Run liner in at controlled speed to minimize pressure surges (approx.12 joints/hr). Check returns

        constantly.

   7.    Fill up liner completely every 10 joints run.

   8.  When the required numbers of liner joints have been made up, make up the liner hanger & liner top

        packer, running tool as per liner hanger engineer’s instructions.

   9.  Use Liner setting tool retrievable pack off assembly for positive cement job.

   10.  Circulate 120% of the liner capacity. (Record Liner weight)

   11. Run the liner with 5” drill pipe to the casing shoe. Circulate a minimum of one string capacity before

       running into open hole, RIH in the open hole at 4-5 min/stand. Liner hanger engineer should be on rig

        floor while running liner into open hole. (Check the loss while RIH if need decrease the speed of the

      RIH).SLM & drift the drill pipe while RIH the liner.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 108

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

Note: before loading the drill pipe wiper plug try to pass it from safety valve.

   12. M/U combination plug dropping head to the last joint. Use pup joint, if necessary. Check flag sub for
       proper functioning.

   13.  The hanger will be set 70m above the 9 5/8” casing shoe. Try to avoid setting hanger on casing
        coupling. Set the Hanger as per hanger engineer procedures.

   14.  Ensure that the running tool is released from the liner (Check liner hanger by move string 0.5m up and
      down – record string lost weight due to set hanger and note in DDR) before starting to cement.

   15.  Circulate 120% of the string contents.

   16.  Cement 7" liner as per cementing program.

   17.  Displace the cement with the cementing pump to the liner wiper plug. Shear out liner wiper plug
       (Record the shearing volume and pressure). Displace combined wiper plugs to latch down collar.

   18. Bump the plug to 3000 psi (Record and report final displacement pressure prior to bump the plug). If
        there is no bump, do not displace more than 25% of the shoe-track volume. Displace with Cementing
      Pump and record cement losses.

   19.  Hold bumping pressure for 15 minutes and Record it as 7” liner integrity test.

   20.  Bleed off pressure slowly and check for back-flow.

   21.  Circulate at least one mud circulation clean the hole. Check the bottom’s up for cement return. In case
        of not available packer, check for U tube f/ DPs. N/D cementing head. POOH to 100 m above Est. top
        of cement, after flush string & Circulate at least one mud circulation, POOH 4 stands, close the well &
       monitor pressure till cement get hard. Open and fill up the well, record mud volume to fill the well.

   22.  Set packer (If available) as per liner hanger engineer instruction.

   23.  Clean out well to liner lap with 8-1/2” bit.

   24.  Circulate and clean hole.

   25.  Perform positive pressure test liner lap to 2000 psi for 30 min.

   26.  If observed NO bumping while cementing, D/O 5m of hard cement.

   27.  Perform Dry test liner lap.

Note:

          - Drift Drill pipe while run the liner with suitable Drift.

          - Perform injectivity test into 7” liner lap if necessary and inject cement around same.

2  20” CASING

  2-1- General
  This section will be drilled from surface to 150 m and will be cemented to isolate the well bore

  and provide a mean to nipple up diverter and control mud return.
  2-2- Cement Formulation and Volume Calculation

2-4- Cementing Procedure
1)  1) Clean up tanks and preparing chemicals for mixing fluid.

2)  Fill tanks with fresh water for mixing fluids.

3) Before finished running casings with company man approval, start mixing cement additives and

    preparing mixing fluids according to below cement additives mixing procedure:

         a. Add Antifoam to fresh water and allow mixing for 5 minutes.

        b. Add CaCl2 and allow mixing for 40 minutes.

         c. Add Dispersant and allow mixing until the cement operation will finish.

4) Before running casing, check the stinger and stab-in shoe & Install stab-in shoe to end of first

    casing.

5)  After running casing, install stinger to end of first 5" drill pipe.

6) RIH to bottom with 5" drill pipe, before setting the stinger, circulation mud.

7)  Set the stinger & circulation mud if observed flowing of mud in casing, try again to setting the

    stinger.

8) Hang drill pipe.

9) Pre job safety meeting with involved personnel.

10) Test cement line W/ 3000 psi.

11) Pump spacer.

12) Mix and pump cement slurry.

13) Displace cement with drilling fluid.

14) Sting out and check for float valve working.

15) Circulate to clean out inner string.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 113

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

16) At the same time the cementer to wash unit and cement line & other.

17) Pull out inner string and lay down stinger.

18) Makes new inventory for cement, additives and equipment.

19) Prepare for next job.

  Note:
    1. The cementing operations information will be recorded by data acquisition system and
      should be submitted.
    2. The cement program may change according to the actual condition.

  3-3- Materials Quantity

    Item       Materials Name                              Unit            Total

      1       Cement Class G                  MT            142

      3      Low Temperature Retarder                 Lbs            419

      4        Dispersant                               Lbs            204

      5        Antifoam                                   Gals            73

  3-4- Cementing Procedure
1)  1) Clean up tanks and preparing chemicals for mixing fluid.

2)  Fill tanks with fresh water for mixing fluids (Lead and Tail).

3) Before finished running casings with Company man approval, start mixing cement additives and

    preparing mixing fluids according to below Lead cement mixing procedure:

         a. Add Antifoam and allow mixing for 5 minutes.

        b. Add Bentonite and allow mixing for 2 hours.

         c. Add Low Temperature Retarder and allow mixing until the cement operation will finish.

4)  Tail cement mixing procedure:

         a. Add Antifoam and allow mixing for 5 minutes.

        b. Add Dispersant and allow mixing for 15 minutes.

         c. Add Low temperature retarder and allow mixing until the cement operation will finish.

5) Before running casing, check cement head specially plunger of released cement plug and put the

    top & bottom plug.

6)  After running Casing, install circulating swage, start circulation at least one casing cap volume.

7) Pre job safety meeting with involved personnel and arrange with mud engineer to measure drilling

    fluid & cement loss/gain during cement job.

8) Stop circulation, connect 13 3/8" cement head and cement line.

9)  Test cement line W/ 3000 psi.

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03)                                             Page 116

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                       NIDC Drilling Projects Management
                              Drilling Engineering & Programming Division

                           South Azadegan Oil field

       NIDC                AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3                                                              PEDEC

10) Pump spacer then release bottom plug.

11) Mix and pump lead cement slurry.

12) Mix and pump tail cement slurry.

13) Release Top plug while pump 2 bbl fresh water by cement unit.

14) Switch to mud pump and start displacement with mud.

15) Displace cement with drilling fluid,  If does not observe Bombing pressure, you will pump

    additional displacement liquid (Drilling Fluid) (25% Shoe track volume).

16) Bump the plug, bleed off pressure and check for float shoe. (If back-flow, attempt cure problem

   by bleeding off and closing in 3 times. If still not holding, hold surface pressure until back flow

    stops.).

17) Makes new inventory for cement, additives and equipment.

18) Prepare for next job.

  Note 1: The cement program may change according to the actual condition.
  Note 2: If Bumping has observed, Increase pressure & hold 1500 psi pressure in CSG for
  15 minute.
  Note 3: The cementing operations information will be recorded by data acquisition system and
   will be submitted.

  3-5- Complementary Top Job
   If the cement does not reach the surface, a complementary cementing job will be done.

 4-4- Single Stage Cementing Procedure

    1) Clean up Mud tanks and preparing chemicals for mixing fluid.

    2)  Fill tanks with water for mixing fluids.

    3) Before finished running casings with Co. man approval, start mixing cement additives and

       preparing mixing fluids according to below Lead / Tail cement mixing procedure:

      Lead Cement mixing procedure:

             a. Add Antifoam to fresh water and allow mixing for 5 minutes.

            b. Add High Temperature Retarder to fresh water and allow mixing for 15 minutes.

             c. Add MicroBlock and allow mixing until the cement operation will finish.

       Tail Cement mixing procedure:

             a. Add Antifoam to fresh water and allow mixing for 5 minutes.

            b. Add Fluid Loss Controller and allow mixing for 15 min.

             c. Add High Temperature Retarder and allow mixing for 15 min.

            d. Add Dispersant and allow mixing for 15 min.

             e. Add Anti Gas Migration and allow mixing until the cement operation will finish.

    4) Before running casing, check cement head specially plunger of released cement plug and put

       the Top & Bottom plug.

    5)  After running Casing, install circulating swage,  start circulation at least one casing cap

      volume.

    6) Pre job safety meeting with involved personnel and arrange with mud engineer to measure

        drilling fluid & cement loss/gain during cement job.

    7) Stop Circulation, Connect 9 5/8" Cement head and Cement line.

    8)  Test cement line W/ 5000 psi.

    9) Pump pre-flush and spacer, then release bottom plug.

   10) Mix and pump lead cement slurry.

   11) Mix and pump tail cement slurry.

   12) Release Top plug while pump 2 bbl Location Water by cement unit.

   13) Displace cement with ------ Bbl drilling fluid, If does not observe bumping pressure, you will

     pump additional displacement liquid (Drilling Fluid) (25% Shoe track volume).

   14) Release Casing Pressure

   15) Monitoring return line for checking Float Collar and Float Shoe.

   16) Rig down Cement Line.

     Note 1: The cement program may change according to the actual condition.

     Note 2: If Bumping has observed, Increase pressure & hold 3000 psi pressure in CSG

      for 15 minute.
     Note 3: The cementing operations information will be recorded by data acquisition system
    and will be submitted.

       If the cement does not reach the surface, a complementary cementing job will be done.
